ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u50	Thank you for your word, chairman. Nice greetings to all present! Today we are deciding on a proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Cooperatives Act. The purpose of the amendment is to modernise the cooperative structures in our country and to enable them to fulfil their mission effectively and transparently. The new cooperative law provides for cooperatives to be able to vote their members remotely, either by means of electronic identification or by mail. The cooperatives will be given the opportunity to sign their records by qualified electronic signature and to cooperate with the members of the administrative or supervisory authority of cooperatives through electronic identification means. We are in favour of these changes in the Modern Centre Party Group, as we are convinced that cooperatives must also follow modern social trends and have the opportunity to use modern technologies. We must not forget that cooperative schemes in our places have had a historically important role, and today, when capital requirements for competitive market participation are quite high, participation in cooperatives for a variety of farmers is the only way to keep pace with competition on the market. This is why modernisation of the traditional system is all the more important as it allows all stakeholders to participate effectively in cooperatives and to devote as much time as possible to their primary mission. We also welcome the anticipated changes that are working towards strengthening the supervision of the cooperative's operations. We believe that it is right for the supervisory authorities to monitor the transparency of cooperatives' operations in a manner similar to that of companies' law. In the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre, we are convinced that the social significance of some cooperatives is much wider than the outlined limits of their operations, so we are particularly inclined to introduce new laws introducing the status of cooperatives with a special meaning, which will identify those cooperatives who, in addition to their basic mission, also carry out the tasks of linking and providing social processes in their geographical area. We are pleased that cooperatives in many Slovenian agricultural environments are once again growing into sovereign communities, giving smaller Slovenian producers the opportunity to break into the market with their crops and products, and gradually becoming centres of social integration in the Slovenian countryside. On the basis of the above facts, we will support the amendment of the law in the Parliamentary Group of the Modern Centre Party. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u52	Thank you for your word, chairman. Secretary of State, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Thank you for your word, again. The proposal for amendments and additions lays down the legal basis for the electronic operation of cooperative bodies. The cooperatives will be able to establish procedures for voting their members by post and also allow them to participate and vote in the general assembly without physical presence by applying for it by means of electronic identification. In order to strengthen the possibility of control of the cooperative’s operations, the Institute of Auditor, which is an audit firm and is comparable to the regulation of a special auditor in company law to carry out an audit of the company’s business, management or individual business, shall also be introduced. It also introduces the status of cooperatives with a particular meaning, which is aimed primarily at cooperatives, which, in addition to their basic function, also carry out the tasks of linking and providing social or other services in their geographical area. It is envisaged that the merger and division of cooperatives procedures should be expedited in such a way as to allow the application for a merger or division of cooperatives to be brought before the Court within seven days of the conclusion of the General Assembly which adopted a decision on the merger or division of cooperatives. With these legal solutions, the bill also brings some editorial changes. In the SAB Group, we support the partnership, because we believe that cooperatives are extremely important for a society, for an economic environment. This is especially true in agriculture. We agree that cooperatives have a great opportunity to become a continuous central supplier of Slovenian trades in agricultural crops and products. Today's amendment to the Cooperatives Act, which is the eighth since the adoption of the Act 20 years ago, aims to strengthen the supervisory and management powers of members of cooperatives in order to protect the assets and activities of cooperatives. Therefore, the Institute of Auditor, which is an audit company and is comparable to the regulation of a special auditor in company law, is being introduced. The auditors may be appointed by the Management Board or the Chair of the cooperative in the last three years for the revision of a particular legal business if the cooperative does not have an administrative board. The epidemic has shown the need for remote access, so the proposal for a new law allows cooperative rules to establish procedures for voting members of the cooperative by post and that members may vote and participate in the general assembly by applying for it by means of electronic identification or other means of access to electronic services in accordance with the rules governing electronic identification. The bill introduces a new cooperative institute with special meaning. This title will be obtained by cooperatives that are more successful in a particular geographical area and have a particular importance for their geographical area. The status of cooperatives of particular importance may also be one of the criteria for obtaining funds from public tenders signed by each ministry or local community within the framework of its approved budget. If we want to be as self-sustaining as possible, we must first and foremost ensure the survival of the Slovenian farmer. The Cooperatives Act defines them as “the organisations of an indefinite number of members who are intended to promote the economic benefits and develop the economic or social activities of their members and are based on a voluntary approach, free exit, equal cooperation and the management of members”. The law lays down the conditions for their establishment, operation, areas of operation and so on. The Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Cooperatives Act extends the said cooperative rights by allowing for the participation and voting of members with an application to the assembly by means of electronic identification. The minutes of the meetings may also be digitally signed after the new one. Surely this change is welcome not only for the special cases we are in, but also for the long term. Members of cooperatives shall be allowed to participate more easily in the bodies and in the management of the cooperative. Modern technical solutions allow the Administrative and Supervisory Committee to participate in both meetings and for signature. For 15 days, the time limit shall be reduced for the cooperative to provide the required information and information to the members and to take a look at its business books and correspondence. Now this deadline has been set for 30 days, which in some cases may be too long. The Institute of Auditor is also being introduced. It shall be appointed by the Management Board or the Chair of the cooperative, but may also be appointed by the majority of the general assembly of the members present or represented. In the event of suspicion of damage to members or serious infringements of the law, the auditor may also appoint an auditor on a proposal of at least 10% of members who are members of the cooperative for at least three years. Audits may be even more important in cooperatives, as they not only allow for a better review of the functioning and possible rationalisations as well as the necessary adjustments, but they can help, in particular regular ones, to strengthen integration and trust within, which is vital for the long-term survival of the cooperative. The status of cooperatives of particular importance is also introduced for cooperatives, which, in addition to their basic function, carry out the tasks of linking and providing social or other services in their geographical area. As such and cooperatives will have a decisive impact on the quality of life of the local population. They will be able to run on various tenders and thus acquire public funds for the purposes of their operation and development. These cooperatives will be decided by the competent ministries according to the scope of the cooperative. Due to more systematic and less biased activities, it might be better to create a single point not only for the register but also for setting up and abolishing and extending the status of cooperatives of special importance. As dispersion as a result of professional competence may be a better choice, it would be better to establish a single point or commission for this purpose in order to facilitate evaluation. It would be better to separate each year's decision on the continued existence of conditions for the status of cooperatives from each other's policy. In particular, the mandate of the current government has shown that any decision can be highly arbitrary, depending on political allies and science. Despite some of the concerns and points raised, we will support the proposed amendment to the Cooperatives Act in Levica.

However, disagreement was expressed with regard to the Committee's proposals for amendments prepared by the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Food, which were taken by the member of the Committee, Branko Simonovic, and with which the information of KMG-MID is withdrawn. KMG-MID is a unique and unique agricultural holding identification number, the use of which is mandatory in all Ministry databases relating to the agricultural economy. It shall be determined upon entry into the register of agricultural holdings. Finally, in Article 9 of the bill, the Government also stated that the information belonging to the agricultural economy is an object and is public. The KMG-MID would allow food traceability in line with the EU strategy, from villas to forks. The number of KMG-MIDs is based on all traceability of food production units or all agricultural production units, which includes land, animals, plant locations, plant protection and so on. The consumer may, on the basis of the entry of the KMG-MID number obtained from the farmer, check on the web browser on which land the food marketed by the farmer is produced. If KMG-MID had remained public, it would have prevented the possibility of speculative sales. In the debate, the Committee adopted proposals for an amendment to the Committee, adopted amendments to the coalition parliamentary groups and articles of the bill.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u84	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. All in attendance, all in attendance! We can view the Agriculture Act and read it as an agricultural constitution, as it is a fundamental agricultural law that determines the consistency, neutrality and equality of agricultural policy measures. Measures and programmes implemented in different areas should be geared towards the same objectives of agricultural policy. The Agriculture Act has been amended and amended six times since 2012, and this amendment is also necessary for the implementation of agricultural policy in accordance with European Union rules and for the implementation of national agricultural policy. More specifically, it specifies who is the beneficiary of agricultural policy measures, complements areas such as the balance of organic substances and nutrients in agricultural soils, the status of agricultural soils and fertilisers, the simplification of administrative procedures for the implementation of agricultural policy and determines the functioning of the Food Supply Chain Relations Ombudsman and redefines illicit practices in the food supply chain. The law brings new developments relating to the control system, the catalogue of infringements and sanctions, new records, imports and preventive and precautionary measures to be taken by organic growers and processors. New organic products such as sea salt, game farming, rabbit farming, insects, wool and leather are added in the area of organic farming. In New Slovenia we stress that production, processing and distribution of safe food are of strategic importance for every society and have a great impact on the economy, environment and health of the population. This was demonstrated strongly at the time of the covida-19 epidemic when the supply of vegetables in the European Union was disrupted due to closed borders between countries. In the area of agriculture, it is important to be aware that the foundations of Slovenian agriculture have always been family farms. In New Slovenia we support the development and preservation of family farms and the transfer of knowledge, work habits, love of home land to young generations. Unfortunately, statistics show that agriculture in Slovenia is in poor condition, more and more outputs from organic farming and abandonment of agriculture in general. From the annual report on the state of agriculture, we can understand that in Slovenia it closes between a thousand and a thousand 500 farms annually. From the point of view of the treated landscape and the conservation of the countryside, this is an alarming information. It is true that the average size of the farm has increased in recent years. If the figure in 2012 was approximately 6.3 hectares, this figure today increased to 6.9 hectares. There are several reasons for closing and closing down farms in Slovenia. Of the most important are certainly increasing and complicated bureaucracy, various records, which are also duplicated, low buying-in prices of agricultural crops and high costs of raw materials and labour, and the lack of young entrepreneurs, as work on the farm is specific, the farm requires the whole person 24 hours a day. We are called upon to return to the farmer the respect and recognition for his efforts and the work he does for all of us and nature. Therefore, Members of the New Slovenia – the Christian Democrats – will be supported by the agricultural law.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u86	Thank you for your word, Vice President. Dear representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues! Today, we are deciding on a proposal for a new Law on Agriculture, which is quite extensive, and it addresses the challenges related to the functioning of individual institutions, the eligibility of operators for agricultural policy measures and coordinates the implementation of certain agricultural activities. The amendment complements areas covering the control of greenhouse gas emissions and establishes a system for checking the quality of agricultural soils. At present, the status of agricultural soils in our country is being monitored only on the basis of physical and chemical analyses carried out on soil samples taken by farmers themselves. However, such control is neither systematic nor ensures that the samples are covered properly. The proposed law builds on the existing way by setting up a structure in the agricultural soil monitoring system and establishing a centre from which data can be collected and managed in an efficient and transparent manner. The drafters of the amendment seek, inter alia, to simplify administrative procedures in the implementation of agricultural policy. To this end, the proposed legal basis is to reduce administrative barriers, which mainly include proof of eligibility in cases where someone is entitled to the funds from agricultural policy measures. Therefore, the necessary data will be searched and verified by the Agency itself and farmers will have fewer problems with bureaucratic processes and will be able to focus on their primary mission. In doing so, it is irrelevant that these data will not be able to be obtained from the Agency by the other institutions, since this protects the legitimate interests of Slovenian producers. Furthermore, this novel provides for the establishment of some new records, records of the standard income of agricultural holdings, records of vegetable and vegetable producers and records of the plant genetic bank. We believe in SMC that a systematic approach to agriculture in Slovenia needs to be deepened. We will provide a good record-keeping system for a review that will allow us to target agricultural policy measures more effectively in those areas that they will really need. The proposal also amends the conditions under which certain public services may be carried out and complements the area of agricultural inspection accordingly. Members of the SMC welcome the efforts made to prepare such a large novelty of law. We are convinced that measures aimed at a higher level of coordination in Slovenian agriculture are welcome. We must all work together to improve the efficiency of the funds we allocate through agricultural policy to Slovenian farmers. At the same time, we must allow farmers to concentrate on their basic mission, which is of paramount importance to our society. We will therefore support the proposal of this new law in the Group of the Parties to the Modern Centre. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u88	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! Compulsory pension and disability insurance is the basis of the social state. The rights arising from it shall be financed from the contributions paid by individuals and from the expenditure of the State budget. According to the Constitution, the State is obliged to take care of the functioning of the pension and disability insurance system and to act with constant but not excessive changes. This is particularly due to the dynamics in social relations where the functioning of the system is threatened by income and expenditure imbalances. In 1996, the Government decided to significantly reduce the contribution rates for pension and disability insurance, the employer's contribution halved. It decreased from 15.5% to 8.85%, while the burden of the employee remained at the same level. The aim was to accelerate economic growth by increasing economic activity, in particular by stepping up the investment activity of companies. The measure also had a marked negative impact, a sharp increase in the liability of the pension fund. Thus, from 1996 to 2000, deficits were created and accumulated. Under the Financial Regulations of the Law on Public Finance and the Law on the Enforcement of Budgets for each year, pension funds were forced between 1996 and 2000 to borrow loans in order to enable them to pay pensions and other entitlements in a smooth manner. In 2001, the capital company's obligation to co-finance the pension fund from its business outcome followed, as well as an important reform novelty. The State budget has assumed the obligation to ensure a stable financial operation of the pension fund by financing the difference between the original revenues of the system and the total expenditure to finance all legal obligations. Such a reduction in labour costs was justified by a new burden on the economy with a tax on paid wages. The revenue of the State budget from the pay tax was highest in 2004 and 2005, 7,5 % of the total budget revenue, 472 million in 2004 and 506 million in 2005. The country gave up this source of revenue in 2009 in the interest of promoting the economic dynamics and competitiveness of Slovenian companies. If this tax type were maintained, it would have an impact on the whole series of macroeconomic aggregates and it is not possible to assess today how much damage this has caused. But clearly there's something else. At the expense of reducing contributions to employers, more than EUR 20 billion was lost, given 2020. This must have been reflected in the amount of pensions. Both pension reforms, on the one hand, increased pension conditions and, on the other hand, reduced the standard of rights previously paid and earned. Opponents of the gradual rise in the rate of contribution of employers refer mainly to the data and the resulting references of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development, the OECD. There, they say that the tax burden of working in Slovenia is relatively high in social security contributions and relatively low in income, but in the legislative procedure there are extensive changes in income legislation that will reduce these burdens. In doing so, it is important to know that we, citizens and citizens of Slovenia, will ultimately be paying for our pension and social system, not bureaucrats from the white world. The regulation of labour-capital relations is the core area of each development vision. Each of these two economic categories is inevitably involved in any reform of tax legislation and solutions are not simple, as is evident in our particular case. Budget revenues are far too low to meet the needs in all state subsystems. Change for the better may be possible, but we will not achieve it until it comes from the belief that the only important processes in society are the economic processes managed by capital and that the power of politicians is generated only by how much capital is raised. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u102	Thank you. Dear Presidents, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The draft law, which is under discussion, amends and complements the Law on corporate income tax, which was adopted in the framework of the 2006 tax reform. Amendments and amendments to the law may be divided into two parts according to content and objectives. Some of the solutions in the bill are necessary to fulfil commitments to respect and transpose EU law into Slovenian legislation, while other amendments and additions represent measures to reduce the consequences of the health and economic crisis in order to create a more conducive business environment, including clear and friendly administrative rules. The proposed measures support other policies, particularly employment policies and environmental policies. Based on these purposes and objectives, the proposal for a law takes into account the EU guidelines on defence tax measures and the EU list of jurisdictions which are not prepared to participate for tax purposes. There is also an article in the directive on anti-tax avoidance rules, for which the time limit for transposition is 31 12. 2021. The bill introduces a new facilitation to promote green and digital transitions and builds up employment facilitation. Beneficiaries will be able to apply the benefits if they employ young people under 29 years of age, persons with a job for which there is not enough staff on the market and those who enter employment for the first time, and if they invest in a green and digital transition. The draft law also lays down powers for sub-legislative acts in order to facilitate the implementation of those provisions. There is an increase in the relief for donations from 0,3 % to 1% of the tax revenue and is expanding in the novel with sports purposes. A higher facilitation is proposed to carry out practical work in vocational education. The proposed tax base measures constitute a more favourable and easier recognition of tax expenditure. Claims declared in the enforcement or bankruptcy proceedings will be better recognised. There is a clearer provision for the recognition of reservations expenditure. A special, more favourable arrangement is proposed for pension reservations, retirement pensions and reservations for jubilee awards in the coming period up to and including 2026. The expansion of incentives and the proposed more favourable definition of the tax base amount to around €27 million per year in the lower revenue of the state budget for corporate income tax. It should be borne in mind that it is difficult to assess the financial effects of certain provisions, in particular those referring to harmful tax measures and practices. In view of the above, the Government proposes the adoption of a decision that the proposal for a law is suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u106	Thank you so much for saying that. The Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Taxation on the Income of Legal Persons represents one of the three laws in the field of tax amendments received by the Government in the National Assembly. The main solutions to the proposal of the Law on corporate income tax are as follows. The basis for determining the list of countries which, from the point of view of fair taxation, pose a risk of abuse is being upgraded. Slovenian legislation will thus transpose the provisions of the European Directive against tax avoidance practices, thus helping to achieve more equitable taxation. A new aid is being introduced to invest in the green transition and to invest in digitisation of 40% of the amount invested. It is about investing in cloud computing, investing in artificial intelligence, environmentally friendly technologies, cleaner public and private transport, and energy efficiency of buildings. There are incentives for donations from 0,3 % to 1 % of the revenue, which means that the debtor can apply a 1 % tax revenue relief to the taxable period of the debtor. The scope of relief is also growing, which means that a higher amount of relief can be used for cultural purposes and for protection against natural and other disasters, even for sport purposes. Facilitating the employment of young, elderly and disadvantaged professions are in place. We are talking about the employment of persons under 29 years of age and over 55 years of age and the employment of persons with a profession for whom there is insufficient staff in the labour market in view of the needs of employers. The amount of relief in the case of employment of such a person shall be set at 45 %. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we really welcome the intention that the areas of the deficit professions should also be addressed through facilitations, as we have been pointing to this issue for a long time. The reasons why we have a large number of deficient professions are mainly in the wrong educational policy and, of course, also in the changing structure of the economy in the world and as a result, in us. On the other hand, youth employment benefits will help young people to get a job in Slovenia and thus stay in Slovenia. There are also incentives for practical work in education from the current 20% to 80%. It is intended for companies that accept apprentices, students or students under the apprenticeship contract to carry out practical teaching work in vocational education, which is of utmost importance for the professional skills of students or students and also for the economy, as the interest in practical learning will increase and, as a result, will lead to better quality of staff and will make it easier for young people to get to first employment. The recognised expenditure shall be increased for the costs of the representative and for the costs of the Supervisory Board or the supervisory authority. This is a partially recognised expenditure which is raised from 50% to 60%. A transitional period shall be introduced to better take account of the tax expenditure on the provision of pensions, pensions and jubilee awards. This is a transitional provision applicable for a period of five years. The proposal for a law on corporate income tax provides for more transparent and fairer taxation, which is crucial for further incentives for economic recovery following the covida-19 epidemic. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will, of course, support the proposal for a law on corporate tax. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u110	Dear Vice President, honourable Members and Members, the rest of you present, welcome again today! Much of the explanation of the law is intended to summarise the European directives which bind us to a related system of corporate taxation. Let us say immediately at the outset that Slovenia has transposed a large majority of all the provisions of the amended European directives in this area into its laws since the previous government. The former governments. In fact, the content involved would require only minimal legal action, which would also impose taxation on so-called hybrid persons in Slovenia. The very purpose of this bill is to enable the Minister of Finance to withdraw from the list of tax havens a number of countries with low tax rates, facilitations and lax controls to enable corporations to massively tax evasion. The adoption of this law would provide the Minister of Finance with a direct legal basis to withdraw from the Slovenian list of tax havens all the 19 countries currently in it. Thus, a short list of countries treated by the Republic of Slovenia as tax havens would be reduced from 26 to only 12 in the jurisdiction considered by the European Union to be those not willing to participate for tax purposes. Among them, there will be no more Bahamas, Barbados, Brunei, Liechtenstein, Maldives and some Caribbean islands that may not be known to the general public, but are very well known by those who transport through them, avoid taxation and hide the origin of their assets. As if this were not enough, the proposal for a law is already greatly expanding a fairly wide range of possible reliefs, which in practice reduces the corporate tax rate well below a generally defined rate of 19 % of that tax. According to the material, after the introduction of the incentives, Slovenian companies pay a real tax on the income of legal persons of an average amount of 13.5%. In 2019 a little over 106 thousand Slovenian taxpayers paid slightly over €906 million. When deciding on taxes, it is important to take into account both the current burden on the taxpayers and the current circumstances, for example, that we need to recover after the epidemic, as well as the need for the country to collect enough taxes to be able to respond to all human needs. This has also been taught by the viral pandemic — the country needs resources to finance everything that people expect of it. The only ones who do not understand this lesson are the Slovenian government and its shadow advisors. At a time when the whole world notes that general corporate taxation is too low and needs to be raised, it is necessary to expand the lists of countries that apply to tax havens and allow tax avoidance, the Slovenian Government proposes a completely opposite direction – a reduction in the tax oasis list and additional reliefs that will already reduce the relatively low tax and budget revenues further. It should be said that overall taxation in Slovenia is indeed slightly higher than global or even European averages, but this does not apply to the taxation of legal persons, capital income or property. For all these categories of taxes, we are already considerably behind averages, and in particular for countries with which we would like to compare. Although different calculations vary according to methodology, all of us are based on international comparisons in a group of countries where corporate taxation is among the smaller ones. However, any additional facilitation of this proposal of the law constitutes a further reduction of this tax without ensuring that it will bring the promised benefits. Many of the new or increased reliefs thus also provide an opportunity for the non-transparent reduction of tax bases and additional tax evasion where capital does not contribute to its share, and the price is paid by people through higher taxes or through the cutting of state services. Ladies and gentlemen, efforts to reach an international agreement on the minimum rate of corporate income tax are also being strengthened during the post-pandemic recovery period. While the new US President is at the forefront of this effort, the coalition of support countries is extremely broad this time. The aim is to ensure a minimum global level of corporate income tax of at least 15% and to pay the tax where the turnover takes place. This would at least partially ease the mass avoidance of taxes, which today is through tax havens or global big corporations, even though it will be the smallest common denominator. It is sad to see that this agreement is being broken by just some European countries. First of all, there are those who today have the very benefit of doing separate business with individual corporations, such as Ireland. If our country takes part in such efforts, this is not good, especially if it chooses a subtle way and disguises tax gifts for businesses as new reliefs for non-transparent purposes, which are supposed to be somehow linked to recovery after shock. It is relevant that the tax rate below 12,5 % in the world and under the applicable rules is considered to be too low. This is the tax rate at which other countries start to include them in so-called blacklists, to deal with them with greater attention and to charge tax revenues to entities operating with such countries. The effective amount of this tax in Slovenia was 13.5% according to data from this legal material in 2019. So only one percentage point above the tax oasis border. In the event of the implementation of all the proposed facilitations, we will be approaching this border very soon. Slovenia, which needs more resources for the needs of its people, is expected to reduce taxes where they are already comparatively lower in a world that strengthens the control of tax evasion and higher corporate tax burdens, as proposed by this government. While other countries are disseminating lists of tax havens, the response of the Slovenian Government, the Finance Minister and its shadow advisors is that some countries cease to be tax havens. While other countries are trying to reach a global agreement on unitary corporate taxation, Slovenia is trying to reach those countries that wish to devalue this agreement quietly. While the circle around the tax lines, the Slovenian Government is kicking itself into this circle. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u117	Thank you very much. The position of the Parliamentary Group of Alenka Bratušek will be presented by mag. Andrei Reich. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u118	Honourable Vice-President, Honourable Secretary of State, Fellow Member and Members of Parliament! For many years, the European Union has pointed out that Member States lose up to €70 billion of tax revenue each year due to multinational practices, which do not pay taxes aggressively where they create them, but where this is more favourable for them. As published in the European Court of Auditors this year, the estimates of revenue lost from avoidance of corporate tax are between €50 billion and €70 billion per year in the European Union. If specific tax arrangements and inefficient tax collection are also included, about €190 billion per year. The amendment is therefore transposed at European Union level in a way that, in addition to the national list of countries with a general tax rate of less than 12,5 %, we also introduce an agreed European list on which tax-risk countries are included. The countries will be included in the so-called European list according to three key criteria: tax transparency, fair tax taxation and implementation of the OECD minimum standards to prevent tax evasion and re-routing of profits. In fact, it is a list of countries which are not prepared to participate for tax purposes. The financial consequences of tightening tax policy in terms of defence against countries with unfair tax practices cannot be assessed in this case, but due to the reduction of the abuse of multiple taxes is expected. In addition to this novel, it brings important news that will have very negative effects on our tax system. The Law on Taxation on the Income of Legal Persons is one of the laws that are part of the so-called tax reforms of the Janša government. It provides for an increase in tax incentives for grants from the current 0.3% and 0.2% to 1% of income. If the novel is adopted, pensions and jubilee awards will be recognised as expenditure at 100%. The recognised costs of representation and of the Supervisory Board are raised from the current 50% to 60%. The legal person who will carry out practical education will, in the new way, have a total of 80% relief. There is a whole new introduction of a green and digital transition facility. The debtor will apply a new reduction of the base by 40% of the amount invested, but not when the funds invested will be obtained either from the state or from the European budget and will have the nature of the grants. The increase in tax reliefs and the introduction of new ones mean that the tax collected will be less. Each relief reduces the basis from which companies have to pay a tax with which the state budget is full. It will cost us €1.5 million this year, €6 million in pensions and anniversary awards, €2.6 million in costs, and €16.5 million in green and investment relief. By adopting this law, we will cut the budget annually by €27 million. In the SAB, we believe that in the current public financial situation, when the state budget is fully exploited, such decisions are reckless, unrealistic, irresponsible and, consequently, fatal to the state budget and to the fragmentation of state subsystems. The fact is that we have spent 4 billion Euros that we did not have and for which we had to take responsibility to combat the epidemic. The fact is, the current government of Janez Janša has historically entrusted us. It is extremely worrying that the state of public finances in Slovenia has deteriorated significantly more than the European Union average. It should be clear to everyone that such an increased volume of debt prohibits any reduction in the revenue of the budget. This government should do exactly the opposite by drawing up measures to drive Slovenia out of the crisis and bringing the state of public finances back to the pre-crisis level. But this government is doing the opposite. Instead of taking care of higher budget revenues, it reduces them. If income is not sufficient, this will mean reducing other public expenditure such as salaries, pensions, social rights and could still be listed. The new law is part of the tax reform of the government of Janez Janša, which will deprive us of billions of euros in budget revenues. In the SAB, we believe that the law is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u120	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! In principle, the State implements three objectives within the framework of tax policy: meeting common and public needs, redistribution of gross social product created and balanced and stable economic development. The challenge of modern tax systems is to provide incentives for accelerated economic development and the basic level of social rights of citizens, while covering the basic functions of the state. The tax system is designed in direct dependence on other subsystems within the broader social, economic system of each country. Tax reform, which will not take into account domestic economic realities, will only increase the budget gap. Political haste creates an ideal environment for new big mistakes. It would be good to keep in mind in all this enthusiasm that the primary objective of tax policy is to fill the state budget, that it does not carry a large budget debt too far into the future and that, ultimately, it prevents the country from going bankrupt. When tax authorities hand over a sparrow to a pigeon on a roof, this is very risky because a tax pigeon rarely, if ever, lands on the ground. Public finances cover the overall structure of instruments and relations governing public funding from budgetary resources. The tax system, the regulatory framework for taxation, the functioning of the State's tax policy instruments are important factors affecting business policy. Under the tax mechanism or under the tax system, the country uses a mechanism of authority and political arrangements based on the redistribution of the power to meet the wider social needs and to correct the market mechanism. The fairness and justification of the State's intervention in redistribution of the social product and in the functioning of the market mechanism is a constant issue and a challenge in shaping the economic policy of a modern country. The regulation of tax policy and tax system in modern societies further complicates the fundamental conflict in the interests of citizens who, on the one hand, want to reduce tax burdens while requiring the maintenance of the achieved scope of public services and social welfare. With economic and technological development, they only step up these requirements. The corporate income tax is, by its size and extent, the fourth most important tax source in the structure of the state budget revenue. In 2019 997 million Euros of revenue under this heading were realised. The proposed changes, which will have a negative impact on income, include the following areas: a new facilitation for promoting a green and digital transition, through the enforcement of which the taxpayer will reduce the tax base for certain qualified investments; a higher facilitation for sport-related donations and a higher facilitation for the implementation of practical training in vocational education; an upgrade of the employment allowance, which is extended through the employment of disabled workers; and a particular provision for the creation of a higher amount of employment relief for young people who are first engaged in employment; a better recognition of the cost of booking, pensions, jubilee awards and retirement benefits, and an increase in the recognised costs of representation and of the supervisory board or body that performs the supervisory function. It all has a negative financial impact, of course. It is estimated at around €27 million. At first glance, this is not much, it is only 2.7% of the realised revenues in 2019. For a more visual picture, however, it is necessary to look at how the Slovenian State dealt with this fundamental tax in the area of direct taxation of the income of legal persons in the past poor decade and a half. This is best seen in the final accounts of the budgets for each year. The corporate income tax was the highest in 2008, one billion and 257 million euros, down to EUR 265 million by 2013. Thus, in six fiscal years, 992 million euros fell, virtually for a billion. Since 2014, these revenues have gradually increased to 468 million and up to 2019, as has already been said, up to 997 million. This is still 260 million euro less than it was realized in 2008. All this time during these 13 years, tax rates, tax reliefs have been widely experimented with, with differences between actual and tax-recognised expenditure. For individual categories, there are, of course, differences that are due, for example, to inadequate consideration of the costs of material, expenditure on sales of stocks of products and commercial goods, depreciation, internal transfer prices, interest, wages, long-term reservations and expenditure on the basis of other expenditure. The sublimation of all these experiments is the fact that the country created a smaller impact in 2019 than in 2008 and we are talking about income from corporate income tax. We can also call this reverse-induced experimentation, and with this swelling we have to stop slowly in Slovenia. Ladies and gentlemen, what is happening in Slovenia today in the field of tax policy is quite similar to what began in the late 1970s and was called the tax revolution. She achieved her full approval within the two mandates of Reagan's administration. Her doctrinal basis was linked to the so-called supply-side school, the economics of the offer. The doctrine promotes tax reduction and mitigation, and this is justified by expectations that economic operators and citizens will be more motivated to work and to invest. As a result, there will be increasing revenues and profits, and thus a higher tax base. The lower tax rates applied to the higher tax base will ultimately increase budgetary revenues. In line with this logic, President Reagan's administration has reduced its tax rates, especially those for the richest ones, and the budget deficit has started to rise instead of falling. The predicted effects have never materialized; the reality has become a preposterous theory, but the policy of low taxes has persisted and has been maintained to this day. All that changed was the argument. The economic virtues of low taxes are now of secondary importance, stressing the urgency caused by globalisation, now and at the moment also by post-epidemic processes. If the state does not increase taxes, then companies may not move production to countries with a more favourable tax policy. There are objectively no conditions for tax reductions and correction of the tax system in different segments is necessary. A progressive tax scale should be introduced, if not for fiscal revenues, at least for the sake of social dignity. Tax discipline must be increased and budgetary spending rationalised, and overall budgetary expenditure will be difficult to reduce if not impossible to reduce. At any moment, we must ask ourselves where Slovenia will finance all projected investments in infrastructure, how it will repay credits and how it will reform the pension system. Members of the Desus Group have different views on the matters described, individually supported, which will prevail in deciding whether the proposed amendments and additions to the Law on the Tax on the Income of Legal Persons are appropriate for further consideration or not. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u142	Dear President, honourable Members and Members of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia and other participants in today's meeting! You have before you a proposal for a law on electronic identification and trust services, as supplemented by the adoption of amendments to the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government on 16. 6. 2021. I will continue to refer to this law with the ZEIZ. The objective of the ZEIZ proposal is to adopt Regulation 910/2014/EU or, in short, the eIDAS Regulation which entered into force 17. 9. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. Seven. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The eIDAS Regulation has re-organized the concept of electronic identity and electronic signature, which we have not yet separated in the Republic of Slovenia. So far, we have used the same digital certificates to identify individuals in electronic commerce as well as for electronic signing, and the issue of digital certificates for electronic signatures is based on a new trust service. Therefore, the ZEIZZ will ensure national legal arrangements for the field of trust services, including the issue of a digital electronic signature certificate, where the regulation allows or allows national procedures and arrangements. However, in the field of electronic identification, the draft law also includes arrangements for the issue of national electronic identity. Let me list below the main points of the ZEIZZ. For the first time in Slovenia, the ZEIZ establishes a system solution for a single electronic identity issued by the Republic of Slovenia to Slovenian citizens and citizens, and under certain conditions also to foreigners on one or more means of electronic identification. Electronic identity will enable citizens to operate electronically and access all electronic services at home, in accordance with the eIDAS Regulation, as well as throughout the internal market of the European Union, as it will enable the user to be identified electronically wherever the identification of a person is necessary for the successful execution of the procedures. The adoption of the ZEIZZ is therefore essential for the inclusion of an electronic identity and a digital certificate for electronic signature on a new ID card. On the basis of the Act on Personal ID, two means of electronic identification of high and low levels of reliability will be found on a new biometric ID card. This will enable citizens to have the electronic identity of a high level of trust, while at the same time allowing the use of a ID card, for example, in the procedures in which we now use the health insurance card. It is important that the new ID card will also be the holder of the digital electronic signature certificate, which will enable electronic signature of documents. On the basis of the ZEIZZ, the Republic of Slovenia will offer through the trust services centre the possibility of electronic public sector service providers, as well as private sector providers registered in the Republic of Slovenia, of using electronic identity and means of electronic identification notified by other countries. ZEIZ also provides a legal basis for electronic authorisation, which will allow individuals to be empowered by the information system to operate electronically on their behalf. A simplified application to the eGovernment State Portal is also added and the identity of the customer is determined on the basis of the user data entered. However, the advanced electronic signature shall be used in the submission of applications through the eGovernment State Portal. Dear Members, the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government is held at 16. 6. 2021 At its 24th meeting, the amendments proposed by the SDS, NSI and SMC members also took into account the comments of the DZ Legislative and Legal Service, which were not of such a nature as to challenge the proposed concepts of the ZEIZZ. I therefore propose that you support the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u160	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! Electronic commerce and electronic signature were legally regulated in Slovenia in 2000 by the adoption of the Law on Electronic Commerce and Electronic Signature. This law has laid down the conditions for the issue and use of electronic signatures and certificates, the legal validity of electronic documents and electronic signatures, the infrastructure of the certifying authorities and so on. 20 years ago, no one could have imagined the dimension and development of digital technology, as today we are having a hard time imagining that most of the applications that we will be using in 10 years are not invented at all. Electronic commerce has become a way of private and public life, covering electronic banking, insurance, marketing and trading, teaching, public sector business, consulting and distance auctions. The degree of development and use of digital services is becoming an indispensable element of the competitive advantages of individual countries in the global economy. The development of technologies and electronic services thus presents new challenges in the regulation, in particular how to overtake all new circumstances within existing legislation. The proposal for a new law on electronic identification and trust services constitutes compliance with the European legal order. The regulation implemented in the proposed law includes two content sets. The first part covers electronic identification where the Regulation does not interfere with electronic identity management systems and related infrastructures of Member States, but merely lays down the conditions under which Member States recognise means of electronic identification of natural and legal persons included in the notified scheme of another country. The bill thus regulates the way towards obtaining electronic identity, allowing or facilitating e-access, procedures and business in both Slovenia and the European Union, and provides for modern, simple and secure electronic identification. The second set covers six trust services, where the European Regulation regulates the entire area and lays down rules for trust services: electronic signatures, electronic stamps, electronic time stamps, electronic recommended delivery services, the storage of qualified electronic signatures and stamps, the certification of the security of electronic signatures and stamps and the certification of websites. Digital identity is today's reality, and its perspectives are all-encompassing. The epidemic only showed in a few weeks where we lack digitalised services and what we could do to make them more widespread, safe, efficient and user-friendly. In the Desus Group, we support the bill. But we remind you of today's digital and digital tomorrow, which poses a major challenge for the elderly, often even an insurmountable obstacle. For illustration only, there are now more than 54 thousand 500 people living in Slovenia who are over 85 years old. Demographic change, which will further change the relationship in the population structure in the future, requires the fullest possible consideration and concrete action at all levels of social development, including in the field of electronic mobility or the digitisation of society. The involvement of older people in these processes is therefore of paramount importance. When introducing a digital way of life, however, we need to introduce digitalisation as an optional way of doing business or lifestyle, not as a categorical, binding or only possible way of doing it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u164	Thank you so much for saying that. Dear President, honourable Members, honourable Members of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia and other participants! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia is in session 22. 4. 2021 adopted a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Public Procurement Act. Hereinafter, I will refer to the bill with ZJN-3B. There were broad public debates, a second amendment, the first amendment proposed by the MPs of the National Assembly, the first amendment of the law prepared by the Government after 2015 when the law was adopted. Just because the law does not change too often, it has allowed the institutes to be tested well in practice. Now, after six years of application of this law, there has also been sufficient time to test and to conclude that certain institutes need to be reformed, especially simplification due to the practice that has been established at that time, as well as the recent epidemic. That is why this law also incorporates some of the solutions brought by intervention legislation. At the same time today, when we have fully switched to electronic communications in public procurement, some tasks are simply no longer appropriate and need to be regulated. Of course, it must be made clear that the public procurement system primarily determines the law of the European Union, where the Slovenian legislature is extremely limited in the legislative regulation of the system, and therefore, in addition to legislative solutions, it is also necessary to seek a different form of legislation. For example, professionalisation is one of them, while the ministry is improving the electronic public procurement system towards complete digital transformation, which will unify procurement and increase competitiveness. If I return to the bill before us. With greater transparency, changes to the ZJN-3B bring greater efficiency and flexibility to the procurement process. However, with the proposed amendments and additions, the law introduces some novelties and exceptions to the promotion, regionalisation and consumption of Slovenian goods, especially for contracting authorities with greater responsibility and added offences, which allow for slightly more room for manoeuvre, particularly when it comes to contracts of small value and municipalities. Here I must also point out that transparency remains ensured in certain solutions that will encourage and accelerate the participation of contracting authorities with the local economy. It is only to allow more flexible and efficient implementation of procedures, which is the most obvious in this segment, that the system is excessively rigid. The bill increases the thresholds for registration orders in order to accelerate procurement and facilitate the balancing of orders to the local economy. These thresholds are followed by intervention legislation. There is also an increase in the thresholds for the use of the low value procurement procedure, which is a fully transparent process and allows greater flexibility and faster implementation of the procedure. In order to simplify the procurement, certain exceptions are added, while legal services are subject to lower procurement thresholds as there is no reason not to apply the procurement rules to these services. On the other hand, transparency in procedures is increasing as the bill provides that all documentation must be published only on the public procurement portal and in all procedures, including in procedures negotiated without publication, a decision on the award of the contract will have to be published. In the case of the use of electronic procurement, however, the law introduces some solutions in cases where the system is not operational. The law also addresses some of the very practical requirements established in the past by practice, for example, that tenderers will no longer have to submit further statements of impunity, and the law introduces a new discretionary right of the contracting authority to eliminate unusually low offers, which is virtually impossible in practice today. In view of what has been said to you, honourable Members and honourable Members, I propose that you adopt the proposed amendment to the law. Thank you very much.

The courts also point to the disproportionateity of the obligatory by-law sanction in relation to the gravity of the offence committed. The proposal is to provide for the most serious infringements of economic operators by the optional expression of the foreign sanction of exclusion from public procurement procedures, while at the same time allowing for a period of time within which the perpetrators are not only punished, but also preventing them from re-applying to the invitation to tender. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u186	Dear Members, honourable Members of the National Assembly, distinguished participants and participants in the meeting! The Earth Book is a public book. As a central city and an instrument for the protection of private property, it is one of the foundations of our social and economic regulation. The regulatory framework governing its functioning should therefore be stable and the intervention should be considered. The bill before you fully follows this approach. These are the first major amendments and additions to the Land Book Act in the last 10 years, but they do not bring about conceptual changes, but are necessary and regular updates of the legal framework to ensure the smooth and coordinated functioning of the system. A set of interventions in the current legal text can be divided into two groups. The first includes changes and additions resulting from laws adopted at the time of the last major novel, which either require appropriate additions to the Land Book Act or are harmonisations that are not necessary for the functioning of the system, but ensure compliance with the legal order. The law, which requires adjustments to the Land Book Act, is a Real Law Code or its last year's novel. It brought into the Slovenian legal order an instrument of related real estates, whose application was delayed until 1 July 2021 by transitional provisions. With this deferred deadline, the legislator wanted to enable what we are here for today at the beginning of application. These are the corresponding adjustments to the Land Book Act, which will enable this new real estate position to be incorporated into the land book and to apply the undeniable benefits that it brings to the owners of the property that will decide to use it. This reason or reason for the amendment of the Land Book Act is also the only one that makes this novel mandatory, since there are no other reasons, such as the implementation of Constitutional Court judgments or the transfer of European law requirements. The second group of interventions to the legal text is the result of a thorough reflection and consultation with key operators of the Land Book Act, i.e. with courts, notaries, lawyers and other professional public. I thank all stakeholders here again for this. During the key interventions of this second group, I should point out the provisions relating to the digitisation of the procedure prior to the authorisation of the land register, i.e. the procedure before the Financial Administration. The Novela provides the basis for the notary to send the contract electronically and also to take over the tax certificate along this same route. This will allow this part of the procedure to be fully digitised and it will be possible to move faster in order to save the parties an additional path and time. The law also lays down a series of other solutions aimed at facilitating the parties to reconcile the land register with the actual one. As an example, I should like to point out a new rule on the deletion of rights that stop the death of persons. Now it will no longer be necessary to look for death certificates if it appears from the birth anniversary that it is not possible for this person to still be alive. Let me stress, this is only about the rights that stop a person's death, not about the rights that are inherited. The provisions governing access to so-called protected alerts shall also be amended in such a way as to allow State authorities direct access to these data, only on condition that they need to do so in order to carry out their duties and powers. The law is also continuing the direction outlined in the amendment to the Judicial Procedure Act to extend intervention with the Supreme Court to all legal areas. This allows the Supreme Court to develop land law in this area by, through the audit, deciding on important legal issues in this area and ensuring the uniform application of law. The law also contains solutions designed to protect creditors and to prevent them from having access to the order of ownership in the event that enforcement takes place over the creditor or that the owner is already prevented from having only possession of the property right. The other amendments introduced by the draft law are related to the terminology adjustments of the Regulation in the Law on State Advocates and the related amendments to the Law on Local Self-Government. The provisions governing the registration of illegal construction under the Construction Law have also been improved. Dear Members and Members, we believe that these sets of amendments and additions constitute a necessary and appropriate intervention in the text of the law. The debate on the working body showed that the bill had been prepared appropriately and that the amendments adopted had been further improved since they addressed all the comments raised by the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, as well as the comments made by the Supreme Court. The same applies to most of the other comments and views forwarded to the Justice Committee [[…]]

The proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Land Book Act is the first major amendment to the law in the last 10 years. The main purpose is to supplement the existing law with rules on new and additional options for registration of property and related properties. This enables the implementation of the Real Law Code. The establishment of these rules should be carried out within the time limit laid down in the Code for the entry into force by 1 July 2021, otherwise these provisions will not be implemented. Consistency with the amendment of the Real Law is required in the field of provisions governing certain issues of property. The additions to the novel provide that a particular part of the building or property intended for the common use of the floor owners of two or more buildings on the floor or the total use of the floor owners of one or more buildings and owners of one or more properties shall be treated as a common part thereof, which is in their co-ownership. The proposal for a novelty for this case provides that, under the registration provisions, the floor property shall be treated in the same way as has been the case so far for other common parts. The novelty reorders the entry of individual real estates both in the main and in the auxiliary real estate. The aforementioned novel of the Real Law Code has also allowed the total property to be shared for the common use of floor owners of two or more buildings or the total use of floor owners of one building or more buildings and owners of one or more properties, such as a multi-block parking lot and a total of one or more residential houses. Such a property is treated as a common part of these buildings or houses and is in co-ownership. In this case, too, entry into the land book is regulated. All provisions relating to land debt shall be deleted from the law after a new provision and the transitional provision shall determine the application of the law in force so far in respect of those land debts still in the Land Book. It replaces state and municipal law with state and municipal law in accordance with the Law on State Attorneys. In order to harmonise the Law on State Lawyerry, an agreement concluded in a peaceful settlement of a dispute between a state lawyer and a counterparty is also included among the instruments underlying the book. Since the proposal for a new law for citizens allows for more transparent, simpler and, above all, more legally secure registrations of property on the ground and allows for the entry into force of the new real estate positions brought by the latest novels of the 2020 Real Law and follow the needs and developments in the real estate sector, we will support the New Slovenia proposal for a new law.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u226	Mr President, thank you for saying so. Dear Members, honourable Secretary of State! You have before you a proposal for a law that we have drafted in order to eliminate discrimination in the eligibility to receive a disability. In accordance with the provisions of the ZPIZ-1, a disability may be acquired by any insured person who has suffered a physical impairment as a result of injury at work or occupational disease or injury outside work, irrespective of whether he has enjoyed any other right under the ZPIZ-1. The invalidity was no longer regulated in the ZPIZ-2. It is only specified that the provisions of PIZ-1 in which the disability is regulated shall continue to apply, but only if the injury is caused by injury at work or by occupational disease. As part of the preparation of the ZPIZ-2 proposal, it was agreed to exclude the disability from the pension scheme. In doing so, in the process of enacting laws, all persons with disabilities who have obtained this right under PPIZ-1 have been provided with the provisions of Article 403, paragraph 3, of the PPIZ-2 to receive it further and the possibility of obtaining the right to a disability only to those whose physical impairment was caused by injury at work or occupational disease. Insured persons whose physical impairment occurred after 1 January 2013 as a result of sickness or injury outside work were thus far unable to obtain the right to disability, as there is no relevant sectoral legislation, although it is expected to be adopted within two years of the entry into force of the PSIZ-2. The aim of the law before you is thus to eliminate the inequality between insured persons entitled to disability due to a physical disability caused by injury at work or occupational disease and those who are not entitled to disability from 1 January 2013 because their physical impairment has occurred outside work. The latter wish to re-enable access to the right to a disability which was no longer available after the entry into force of the ZPIZ-2. An even more important aim of the law is to eliminate the inequality between insured persons who have a physical disability arising out of work before 1 January 2013 and those who for the same reasons have a physical impairment after that date. Because there is no personal difference in these persons, inequality is even greater from this point of view. Article 403 and Article 429 of the Law shall be deleted from those parts of the Decisions which contain the condition that the damage is caused by injury at work or by occupational disease. This allows all insured persons with disabilities, irrespective of the cause of their origin, to obtain the right to payment of the invalidity allowance. We are pleased that the proposal for a law to be considered by the competent Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons Committee has received unanimous support, and we also welcome the amendment adopted by that Committee, which addressed the concerns of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly. We are aware that the law is non-systematic, but we hope that its adoption will temporarily fill the legal void and eliminate discrimination between individuals who differ only after the time and cause of the occurrence of physical impairment and their needs are completely comparable. This is about people whose income as a disabled person is crucial to ensuring their involvement in social life, and I ask you to support the proposal for a law. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u230	Thank you, President, for your word. Dear Members and Members! In September 2020, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia received an opinion on the Proposal for a Law amending and supplementing the Law on Pension and Disability Insurance, which was submitted to the National Council. The proposal proposes that the right to disability be reintroduced in PIZ-2 also in respect of insured persons who suffer from injury outside work and disease. In the case of disabled persons, PPIZ-2 retained the rights already enjoyed by individuals on this basis until their application and ensured their continued payment, while allowing the right to disability to continue to apply the rules on the protection of persons with disabilities, which are intended to regulate the procedures for determining the type and degree of physical impairment, can only be obtained for those physical impairments resulting from injury to work or occupational disease. Since the relevant rules were not adopted within the prescribed time limit, the Council of State has given a legislative initiative to amend the PPIZ-2 regime in a way that would allow the right to disability to be regained until disability rules are implemented, including for physical defects resulting from injury outside work and disease. The proposal also allows new rules on types and degrees of physical impairment to be applied to the recognition of rights if adopted in the meantime. The government has examined the indications and arguments of the bill's proponents and considers that the proposal does not address the issue in a manner that follows the objectives and principles it stresses, otherwise, because the issue is not regulated equally for all individuals in the same situation. The proposal re-establishes unequal treatment between individuals with physical impairment. PPIZ-1, in conjunction with PPIZ-2, regulates the possibility of obtaining a disability only in the case of a existence of insurance, which means that without that right individuals who may also be given an equal or worse disability, which occurred at a time when the individual was not insured. The Home Committee for Work, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons supported the proposal of the law when adopting smaller supplements. The effect of amended Article 403 of the Act is thus bound by the existing provisions of this Article to the time following the entry into force of the proposed amendment of the Law of ZPIZ-2J, allowing for the acquisition of the right to disability from the first day of the next month following the submission of the request and for a maximum period of six months. Finally, from the point of view of the Government, we would like to add that we agree that a new list of types and degrees of physical impairment as well as the relevant rules in the field of persons with disabilities should be adopted as soon as possible, but in the manner originally foreseen by the legislator, that is to say, independently of insurance and only depending on the type and degree of physical impairment, which would establish a single system of recognition of rights on this basis and comprehensive solutions that will treat individuals equally in the same situation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u232	Thank you for your word. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u234	Thank you for your word. Nice greetings to all present! The New Law on Pension and Disability Insurance, which we are considering today at the proposal of the Council of State, is proposing only one solution. By deleting the provisions containing the condition that the disability is caused by injury at work or by occupational disease, the right to disability shall be reinstated also for those insured who have suffered physical impairment as a result of injury outside work or disease. The invalidity shall be in accordance with the current arrangement of the monetary remuneration to which an individual is entitled in the event of a physical impairment. It is regulated in PIZ-1, although the latter is no longer in force and is still in use in this part. In accordance with the current regulation, a physical defect is reported if the insured person has a loss requiring greater efforts to meet the needs of life, whether or not this defect causes disability. An insured person who develops a disability during insurance shall be entitled to a disability pension under the same conditions as those applicable to the acquisition of the right to a disability pension. ZPIZ-1 divided the body's damage into eight degrees. These are only one of the criteria on which disability is then measured. However, the second criterion is related to the cause of physical impairment. The invalidity resulting from an illness or injury outside work shall be 70 % of the amount fixed for a physical impairment of the same degree resulting from injury at work or occupational disease in accordance with the second paragraph of Article 146 of STIZ-1. If the physical impairment is partly due to injury at work or occupational disease, and partly due to disease or injury outside work, the disability shall be administered in total according to the overall degree of disability found. However, the amount of disability shall be determined in proportion to the impact of the individual cause on the overall percentage of disability. It is true that, in accordance with the current arrangement, the disability is regulated in the form of a compensation for physical impairment obtained during insurance or at the time of retirement, and constitutes the right to a long-term and periodic benefit paid monthly to the insured or the user. It is also true that this is an additional right from compulsory disability insurance and not a right from disability insurance. As such, therefore, it is an independent and inalienable right. This right is independent of the possible simultaneous enjoyment of other rights by law. There is also no condition that disability is caused by physical impairment or disease. It is particularly important that the acquisition of the right to disability is not linked to the beneficiary’s property situation. This is a non-contributory income, which is one of the basic principles of pension and disability insurance. Despite the clear purpose of the Institute of Disability, a significant proportion of individuals lost this right in 2013, although the difference between beneficiaries is only due to the occurrence of physical impairment. The Ombudsman has been warning us about anomaly and discrimination in the current regulation for a long time. He has repeatedly called on the competent ministry to eliminate discrimination which has been in force since 2013 and which has rendered the right to disability no longer recognised to all persons with disabilities with a specific disability. On the home working body, we supported the proposal of the law in the majority, as well as the supplement relating to the use of an already existing list on types and degrees of physical impairment. We only wish that the preparation of a new list on types and degrees of physical impairment would not take too long. We will support the bill in the Group of the Modern Centre Party. Thank you.
