However, it was clear to some social workers that these two rights were not excluded, because they were different for the purpose and by the beneficiaries. As a result, some parents turned to the court. In 2012, the Supreme Labour and Social Court decided that the right to an allowance for the care of a child is the right of parents and of an indirect child, while the right to an allowance for assistance and service is a personal right acquired by blind persons, i.e. children. However, it is a matter of direct concern that only three years later, the competent ministry of the Centre for Social Work has notified this information. No one knows why this kind of practice. The Ombudsman also warned for years that the child’s care and support and service allowances are not excluded, but are based on a different factual and legal basis. The Ombudsman also took action to pay back the injustice and drew attention to due regard in the exercise of rights from public funds. The consequences of errors of public authorities or holders of public powers cannot simply be passed on to individuals. Three years ago, we also discussed solving the problems raised by the Commission on Petitions, Human Rights and Equal Opportunities. The proposed law therefore regulates today the payment of unpaid aid and service allowances to which families who have in the past decided to apply an allowance for the care of the child and not an aid and service allowance. Thus, a new person will be entitled to payment of an unpaid allowance for aid and service if, at any time during the period between 1 January 2003 and 31 December 2016, in accordance with the then applicable legislation governing pension and disability insurance, he fulfils the conditions for obtaining an allowance for assistance and service and has not received it because one of his parents has received an allowance for the care of the child. The right to pay the outstanding aid and service allowance will be recognised to the beneficiary for the period during which he met the conditions mentioned above. For 2018 and 2019, the allowance for aid and services for blind persons from 1 January 2019 onwards shall be EUR 300 and this amount shall be due to the beneficiary for each month when the conditions for receiving the aid and service allowance have been met and he did not receive it. The proposal for a bill on the payment of the unpaid allowance for assistance and service will be supported by the Modern Centre Party Group. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u258	President, thank you for your word. Hello again! In 2003, the novelty of the pension law came into force, on the basis of which the social work centres, based on the opinion of the Health Insurance Institute, requested parents to choose between the child care allowance and the allowance for assistance and service. Nevertheless, some social work centres paid both allowances to their parents, and the State later claimed the parental contributions received as a creditor. This has ruled that the right to an allowance for the care of a child under the Parental Protection and Family Benefits Act is the right of parents and indirect children who need special care and protection, the right to an allowance for assistance and service under the Pension and Disability Insurance Act is a personal right acquired by blind persons, i.e. children. Not long ago, we adopted a similar marriage in this house. The Child Care Allowance Act has addressed those families who have decided to apply the aid and service allowance, and today the other side of the same coin is ahead of us, and we recognize the right to those who have decided to apply the child care allowance and not to enforce the aid and service allowance. Thus, for the period between 1 January 2003 and 31 December 2016, beneficiaries will be paid, for each month, when they met the conditions for payment of the aid allowance and the service for the blind, an amount of the aid allowance and a service of EUR 300 free of late interest will be paid to the beneficiaries. The right is exercised at the Institute of Pension and Disability Insurance of Slovenia, which will also pay the latter. The estimated financial impact in 2021 is €1.5 million. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government of Aleš Mihelič, State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u276	Dear Presidents, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Allow me to give a brief explanation for the law on the promotion of the use of renewable energy sources. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia has drawn up a proposal for a new law on the promotion of the use of renewable energy sources which transposes the provisions of the new Directive 2018/2001 on the promotion of the use of renewable energy sources. The Law on the Promotion of the Use of Renewable Sources of Energy provides substantively for the implementation of the policy of the State and the municipalities in the field of the use of renewable energy sources, sets targets in the field of renewable resources and measures to achieve these objectives and ways of financing them. The law also regulates a certificate of origin, self-supply with electricity and renewable resources, renewable energy communities, renewable energy use in the heating and cooling sector and in the transport sector. The current area of renewable resources is governed by the Energy Act, EZ-1. In addition, frequent amendments to European legislation, and the increasingly detailed and extensive regulation of sectoral matter, are increasing the scope of the content covered by the EC-1 and thus the need to constantly amend the existing law. Due to its scope, the matter regulated by EZ-1 became increasingly opaque. It was also becoming increasingly difficult to ensure timely transposition of the directives into the Slovenian legal order. For this reason, it has been shown that there is a need for the legislative regulation of energy to be divided into a number of separate laws following the structure of the legal regulation of these contents in the secondary legislation of the European Union. This is also followed by a proposal for a law that excludes the field of renewable energy sources from EZ-1 and regulates it independently. The basic objective of the bill is to promote the use of renewable energy in all sectors of energy production and consumption. We are therefore pursuing the objective of climate neutral society and the greening of our economy and society. Some major changes to the law. The Act sets out an annual starting share of the EEE, which must not be less than 25 %, while the overall 2030 target and the interim technology targets are set in the NEPN. The law regulates the support scheme and in this context also the content relating to declarations for production facilities and certificates of origin. The law also provides for the operation of a support centre, i.e. a compulsory public economic service operating funds. On the basis of the concession, Borzen will continue to perform this service. The law also provides for compensation to local communities for the use of wind room. In the area of self-sustainment, there are significant changes in relation to the existing scheme. The final customers will have to pay the network for all the energy they take from the network, while at the same time they will be paid for the surpluses. From the point of view of spatial regulation, it is stipulated in the law that planning operators and network operators must take into account the promotion of EEE in developing and positioning. A contact point shall also be established to assist the applicant in the process of obtaining licences. In the area of heating and cooling is a new ban on the design and construction of boilers on heating oil, lubricants and coal after 2023. In the case of remote heating systems, the objective is to increase the share of EEE by 1% per year, the obligation to draw up sustainable plans and the possibility of disengageing the customer from energy-efficient systems after 2025. In the case of the integration of renewable energy into the market, it is stipulated that up to 2030 a share of at least 14 % from renewable sources will be achieved and the share in each year must not fall below 10 %. The adoption of the law on the promotion of the use of renewable energy is therefore crucial for the implementation of measures in this field and for the achievement of the objectives of the Republic of Slovenia. Thank you very much for your attention.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u277	Thank you very much. The proposal for a law was addressed by the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space as the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to Vice-President Mateja Udovč. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u278	Your Honor! The Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space is at its 30th session. 6. 2021 As a parent working body, the proposal for a law on the promotion of the use of renewable energy sources submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for its consideration and decision following a regular procedure. In addition, on behalf of the applicant, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure Blaž Kosorok pointed out that the basic objective of the bill is to promote the use of renewable energy in all sectors of energy production and use, thus pursuing the objective of a climate neutral society and the greening of our economy and society. As a key substantive solution, he pointed out, inter alia, that the draft law sets out an annual baseline share of renewable energy sources, which must not be less than 25%, while the overall target by 2030 and the interim targets by technology after years are set in the comprehensive national energy and climate plan. Substantial changes are taking place in the area of self-sustainment according to the existing scheme, as final customers will have to pay the network for all the energy they take from the network, while at the same time they will be paid for the surpluses. Payments of the proportional share of the contribution to renewable energy resources and the contribution to energy efficiency will be exempt. Among other things, the time limit for the duration of the authorisation is set for a total of not more than two years and for production plants up to 150 kW for a maximum of one year. In the case of the integration of renewable energy into the market, it provides that by 2030 a share of at least 14 % of renewable energy sources will be achieved and that the share of each year must not fall below 10 %. The representative of Valentina Marolt's Legal and Legislative Service explained that they had made comments to a certain article in a written opinion, which were duly taken into account by the amendments of the coalition groups. With regard to the definition of two terms, a proposal for an electricity supply law was subsequently introduced, in which both terms are also clarified. The Commission of the National Council for Local Self-Government and Regional Development supports and, inter alia, assesses that the proposed legal solutions are appropriate and notes positively that the Ministry of Infrastructure has previously coordinated the proposal with all three representative associations of municipalities. In the short discussion, several comments were made by the Levica Group on the content of the draft law, inter alia, as regards the provisions of Article 4, paragraph 3, whereby each type of electricity production from renewable sources and the construction and acquisition of facilities and land needed for it would be of public benefit. In their view, increasing the capacity to produce renewable energy is essential to reduce greenhouse gas emissions, but this cannot always be of public benefit as renewable energy sources can also have many negative environmental and social consequences. Thus, the reckless installation and construction of renewable energy production capacities, in particular the construction of large hydropower plants, can be devastating for many animal and plant habitats. Similarly, the construction and production of renewable energy resources cannot be of public benefit when it has a significant impact on other sectors of the economy, such as tourism, and reduces the quality of life of the inhabitants and residents in their surroundings. Among other things, they objected to the provision of Article 13, paragraph 20, which provides that, in order to maintain the sustainability of the financing of the support scheme, the Government may limit the annual installed power of the production facilities to renewable energy sources which may be supported if the proportion of such production facilities exceeds the planned volume of installed installations in the national energy and climate plan adopted for that year. In their view, this is unacceptable as an increase in the share of renewable energy sources is one of the key conditions for achieving carbon neutrality. After discussion, the Committee adopted the amendments of the coalition groups to Article 8 of the bill and then voted on and adopted all the articles of the bill together. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u287	Thank you very much. The next is Mr Franci Kepa, who will present the position of the SDS Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u288	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Nice greetings to the secretary, colleagues, colleagues! The proposal for a law on the promotion of the use of renewable energy sources with regard to the transition to a carbon neutral society is one of the very important laws, as it will pave the way for this objective. The purpose of the law on the promotion of the use of renewable energy sources, the EES, is to harmonise the national legal order with European Union law. The law transposes the legislative acts of the Clean Energy package into the Slovenian legal order for all Europeans. The Clean Energy package provides for the transition of the European Union to a carbon neutral society by the end of 2050. To achieve this objective, the OVE Directive sets a common target by the end of 2030, achieving at least 32 % of OVE in gross final energy use in the European Union countries. The basic objectives of the bill are: to increase the use of renewable energy sources in the production of all kinds of energy and also in propulsion fuels; to reduce greenhouse gas emissions; to transpose the requirements of Directive 2018/2001 into the Slovenian legal order; to update the system for promoting the use of renewable energy sources in all areas of production and use of energy. The other objectives of the draft law relating to the basic objective are to: to renovate the system of support for the use of renewable energy sources and to extend it to other types of energy; to regulate in more detail joint renewable energy projects with other Member States as well as with third countries; to regulate self-sustainment of electricity from renewable energy sources in more detail; to regulate communities in the field of renewable energy production; to achieve certain administrative simplifications and to establish a single point of contact for renewable energy projects; to integrate renewable energy into heating and cooling; to establish stricter requirements for propulsion fuels in transport. The bill will have certain implications in the field of public administration and will simplify the communication of investors as parties to the proceedings, as they will only communicate with a single contact point. The proposal will also have a favourable impact on the environment. The positive impact of the bill's proposal on the economy is expected due to new investments in production facilities on renewable sources and electricity storage facilities from these sources. We will support the draft law in the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you for your attention.

If anyone thinks that recruits will be able to complete a professional soldier's gap, he's wrong. In fact, recruiters are not intended to carry out combat tasks or to be sent to missions, but can only be a complementary element in the event of emergency situations in the event of war. In the Group of Non-Aligned Members, we therefore propose that we return to the beginning of this topic, to the question of what is Slovenia's greatest threat. According to our assessment and assessment of the professions, cyber attacks and organised crime are the first place among the threats, followed by threats of radicalisation in society, but none of these threats can be adequately addressed by the recruitment army. We therefore propose that these funds be diverted to the management of cyber and other contemporary threats and to improve the status or social position of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces, which would make the profession of soldiers significantly more interesting and even more competitive in the labour market. Furthermore, it is recalled that for such a complex project as the introduction of recruitment, at least basic staff, material, infrastructure, logistics, financial and other resources should be provided. But the Defence Minister himself admitted at the last meeting of the Defence Committee that they do not even have accommodation facilities for all the boys and girls who applied for voluntary military service this year, so some will be forced to cancel. To conclude, I would like to point out that changing the way in which the army is complemented will certainly not contribute to greater security for our country and its citizens. According to past experience, recruiters have not contributed to the greater fighting capacity of the Slovenian Army. We must not forget what problems we have had in the past with the health of the generation of recruits, that we are not even talking about finding different excuses to avoid this. At the same time, however, we need to know that military service in Slovenia can already be carried out in several different ways in the current system, namely, first in the permanent composition of the Slovenian Armed Forces, second in the form of voluntary military service, third in the form of a voluntary contractual reserve, fourth in special circumstances, following the activation of the concept of the military strategic reserve in this structure. As it is said, we do not favour the proposed law in the Group of Non-Aligned Members. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u314	Madam Vice-President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! In this mandate, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has adopted a series of measures to regulate the situation and further develop the Slovenian Army. Last year, this National Assembly, on a proposal from the Government, adopted the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Defence Act, which dealt with some open issues of service in the contractual reserve composition of the Slovenian Armed Forces and, in particular, the status of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces after an additional 45 years. Furthermore, on a proposal from the Government, the Act on the provision of funds for investments in the Slovenian Army was adopted between 2021 and 2026, which arranged for the modernisation and equipment of the Slovenian Armed Forces during that period. The concept of a single entry into the Slovenian Army, which began this month on 5 July, was introduced with the deployment of more than 120 volunteers for military service. This set of measures also includes the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Slovenian Army Service Act, which is currently on your hands, which aims at: to regulate the issue of working time and rest of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces at the time of their deployment to international operations and missions; to further regulate the conduct of military service and fundamental relations in its implementation, as well as the special rights, obligations and responsibilities of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces during the course of this service; to further regulate the specificities of the legal character arising from the specificity and responsibilities of the military service for which the experience of the completion and operation of the professional army has shown that they are not properly regulated; to increase the interest in employment in the Slovenian military and to ensure the planned retention of personnel and to prevent the reduction of the cadre composition of the Slovenian Armed Forces. Let me mention only the most important solutions proposed in this law. Unlike general legislation, the required level of education for a fifth-class soldier is regulated for which at least secondary vocational education is required. It is an oversight of the level of education, whereby the soldier can redistribute and increase only within the status of the soldier, that is to say, to the rank of Corporal. The issue of scholarships is further addressed. As a measure to obtain candidates for employment in the Slovenian Army, a legal basis is created for financing the acquisition of secondary vocational or professional education to a candidate who has concluded a contract on voluntary service of military service. Weekly rest in international operations and missions is being re-organized. The amended regulation is necessary, I stress the urgency, due to the high number of complaints and claims made by members for compensation due to unused days of weekly rest in international operations and missions and relevant case law. In order to evaluate the total prohibition arising from the right to strike, military personnel are provided with a salary allowance of 4% of the last average monthly gross salary in the Republic of Slovenia. There is a new definition of the possibility that a member who has been employed in the Slovenian Army for more than 10 years and who has been a tenant for more than three years may apply for the purchase of this apartment if the tenant and the users of this apartment are the only apartment. They define the additional rights of members of the family members of the Slovenian Armed Forces who lost their lives in carrying out the military service, namely the advantage of their children in entering kindergarten and the reimbursement of the costs of kindergarten. It is arranged to allow immediate family members to visit a member who has been injured or injured or ill in an international operation or mission by seriously endangering his life. Transportation to accommodation can also be carried out by military transport and accommodation can also be organised in military facilities. Regarding the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, amendments were tabled to the coalition, which are editorial in nature and also endorsed by the Defence Committee. In addition, we have made extensive additional explanations, explanations and views, which, in our view, adequately explain and respond to the dilemmas and comments put forward by the Legislative and Legal Service in its opinion. Honourable Members of Parliament and Members, the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Service Act in the Slovenian Army covers a set of measures to regulate the status of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces and to increase interest in entering the various structures of the Slovenian Armed Forces, from the contractual reserve structure through voluntary military service to the permanent composition of the Slovenian Armed Forces. The measures taken so far in practice already give first results in an increased interest in integrating into various military structures as well as in updating and equipping the Slovenian Armed Forces. With the support of the law before you, you will support efforts to continue its development in the direction of a modern and effective organisation capable of carrying out its tasks, ensuring the country's adequate defence capacity and facing modern security challenges after a long period of life. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u316	Madam Vice-President, thank you for the floor. Dear Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! The Service Act in the Slovenian Army is one of those laws that regulate in more detail the rights and duties of Slovenian soldiers, members and members of the Slovenian Army. Among the key solutions, he pointed out that, unlike general legislation, the required level of education for a fifth-class soldier is governed by minimum secondary vocational education. It is an oversight of the level of education, whereby the soldier can redistribute and increase only within the status, that is to say, to the rank of Corporal. Among the key solutions, the Minister also highlighted the 4% ban on the average gross salary and the provision of adequate rest for members and members of the Slovenian Armed Forces serving in international operations and missions. The additional law also regulates the issue of scholarships, and it is proposed to shorten the obligation of fellows to enter into a fixed-term contract after school, which must not be shorter than the duration of the scholarship. Novela also regulates the resolution of the housing problem for active and retired members and members of the Slovenian Army. At the same time, it also regulates compulsory supplementary pension insurance and payment of contributions in the absence of work due to injury at work or occupational illness. For the Chief of General Staff of the Slovenian Armed Forces, it provides, inter alia, for the possibility of early retirement without lowering the pension. Representative of the Legal and Legislative Service mag. In its opening remarks, Andrea Kurent pointed out that the Legislative and Legal Service had examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the legal system and the technical and legislative aspects and had commented on most of the articles. It further highlighted the inconsistencies in the draft law from a constitutional, systemic and nomological point of view. In the debate, the panellists from the coalition parliamentary groups supported a bill that they thought should have been adopted much earlier. They pointed out that the proposal for a law meant the continuation of the necessary regulatory changes, which would increase the attractiveness of the military profession and consequently improve the staffing structure of the Slovenian Armed Forces. The opposition MPs were more reserved for the bill. In the debate, they agreed that some of the solutions were indeed intended to improve the status of a member of the Slovenian Armed Forces, but that they should be placed within the appropriate legislative framework so that the legislative proposal would not become the subject of a decision in the Constitutional Court. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted amendments to Articles 1, 6, 8, 10, 15 and 32 of the draft law by the SDS, NSI and SMC. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended draft law is an integral part of this report. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u322	Thank you so much for saying that. Nice greetings to all present! Amendments and additions to the Service Act in the Slovenian Army are prepared to regulate the working time and rest of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces during deployment to international operations and missions, to increase interest in employment in the Slovenian Army by improving the conditions, and to harmonise the law with the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Defence Act. In other words, the aim of this government is to achieve its objectives in order to increase its interest in military service, motivate any new members and, of course, strengthen the Slovenian Army. The main solutions to the law or amendments are: from three to five years, the time will be extended when the required level of education for the fifth-year military is to be increased to a direct higher level, with the aim of facilitating career paths and fair progress; otherwise, the required level of education for the fifth-graduate soldier, for whom a minimum of secondary vocational training is required, is proposed to reduce the obligations of the fellows after completion of their studies when they have been enrolled by the Ministry of Defence, the contract of employment in the permanent composition of the Slovenian Army is to be concluded for a certain period of time, which must not be shorter than the duration of the scholarship. There is also a reduction in the time when the scholarshiper must remain in service in the Slovenian Army reserve after the end of the scholarship. As a measure to obtain candidates for employment in the Slovenian Army, a legal basis has also been created for financing the acquisition of secondary vocational or professional education to a candidate who has concluded a contract on voluntary service of military service. Exceptionally and restrictively, the possibility of creating a multiple name for certain formational duties has been introduced, which has so far only been applied to specialist works or tasks. Then there is a change in the regulation of the right to rest in international operations and missions, which provides for a concentrated use of weekly rest and full exclusion of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces from the working environment in international operations and missions. This will allow for continuous 24-hour rest and adequate regeneration of members of the Slovenian Army on missions. In cases where it is not possible to provide weekly rest at international operations and missions for operational reasons or the nature of the work, a new member will be entitled to one day of special leave during the seven consecutive days. We have also made some good proposals in the Slovenian Democratic Party or in the parliamentary group, which are now included in these amendments to the law and in this law, and we are pleased that the Government is taking a vision of the development of the Slovenian Army, that it does not give up and is successful in turning the long-standing negative trends in the Slovenian Army for the better. The proposal is good, so we support the law in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group. It is known that we also support the Slovenian Army all the time. All stakeholders who are concerned that Slovenians can sleep peacefully, thank you. Thank you very much to every soldier and soldier. Thank you.

Here we think mainly of members and members of the Slovenian Armed Forces, who, in addition to training to carry out military tasks, in every case of natural and other disasters, always jump unselfishly to help save people and their property. The proposed addition shall govern the status of a military person after the termination of the duties of the Chief of Staff of the Slovenian Armed Forces. This is therefore the highest military formational duty that a military person can occupy, and it also represents the top of the military career path. We do not see any argument by the Social Democrats that, in view of the military hierarchy, it would not be appropriate for a military person to continue to work in the military structure after the cessation of his duties as Chief of Staff of the Slovenian Armed Forces. The proposed solution would be to the refugees in our regulation, since no profession or professional group has such a privilege. In addition, there would be duplication of rights as military personnel are considered to be receiving additional financial resources in the pension pillar, which also allow them to retire under the system of compulsory supplementary pension. We believe that there are already formational jobs within the military organisation to which former chiefs could be deployed, thus making use of their rich knowledge. The possibility of early retirement without a reduction of pension, as provided for in Article 38 of the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, would otherwise be possible if there were no possibility of transferring such a person to appropriate work at the Ministry or in another State body. We are convinced that the former commissioners can be properly redeployed within the Slovenian Army, the Ministry or other state bodies. We would also like to draw attention to Article 28 of the amendment, which is a new article 89a, where additional rights are introduced for an unemployed spouse. We believe that it would not be fair for spouses of other public servants who accompany partners abroad to have this privilege only members of the Slovenian Army. Such arrangements should apply to all partners of public servants abroad. As I have already mentioned, the proposed solutions in the amendment to the Service Law in the Slovenian Army are going towards the resolution of some of the situations in the Slovenian Army, so the Social Democrats will not oppose the proposed amendment of the law, despite some concerns. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u327	Thank you very much. The next is Mr Andrej Černigoj, who will present the position of the NSI Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-12-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u328	Madam Vice-President, thank you for your input, for the third time. Dear Secretary of State, the esteemed choir! The Service Act of the Slovenian Army, which was adopted in 2007, achieved positive effects in the initial phase in the acquisition, award and retention of personnel. As a result of the financial crisis and financial constraints in the defence sector, the positive effects of the law on remuneration, the acquisition of personnel after 2010 have virtually been eliminated. The number of members of the permanent composition of the Slovenian Armed Forces began to decrease and it was no longer possible to ensure the planned extent of completion of the permanent composition. Between 2007 and the end of 2020, the number of permanent members decreased by 686 members and members. We want to make the profession of Slovenian Armed Forces more attractive to every citizen of the Republic of Slovenia by law on service in the Slovenian Army. We therefore propose short-term obligations for scholarships to conclude for a certain period of time, which may not be shorter than the duration of the scholarship, after the completion of their studies instead of twice the duration of the scholarship. There is also a reduction in the time when the scholarshiper must remain in service in the Slovenian Army reserve after the end of the scholarship. An important novelty is the change in the regulation of the right to rest in international operations and missions, followed by MOM, which envisages the concentrated use of weekly rest and the full exclusion of members of the Slovenian Armed Forces from the other working environment in MOM, allowing for continuous 24-hour rest and adequate regeneration of members. Where, for operational reasons or the nature of the work, it is not possible to provide weekly rest in MOM, a special leave institution shall be introduced, after which a member will be entitled to one day of special leave within seven consecutive days, a special leave shall be used in MOM following a decision of the supervisor and in accordance with the needs of the service. If this is not possible, the member will benefit from the leave immediately after arrival with the MOM or no later than two months after returning home. Additional rights of family members of the Slovenian Armed Forces who have lost their lives in carrying out military service are also provided for, namely, their children's advantage in entering kindergarten and reimbursement of the costs of kindergarten. Serving in the Slovenian Army is patriotic and specific. The prohibition of strike is clearly set out in the Defence Act and therefore a salary allowance of 4 % is provided for military personnel. With the acquisition of the Ministry of Defence, New Slovenia has turned the trend of the Slovenian Armed Forces upwards, as the difference between the number of departures and the number of arrivals to the Slovenian Army has decreased significantly. We have restored dignity to members and members of the Slovenian Armed Forces with the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Defence Act, where systematic measures have been taken to allow the extension of the contract for the employment of soldiers in the Slovenian Army on certain forms of duty even after the 45th year and the possibility of employment following the expiry of the contract in the Slovenian Army in other national authorities under significantly more favourable conditions. This law has also taken measures to improve the performance of the contract reserve composition of the Slovenian Army. For the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats is an important part of Slovenian society, so we will unanimously support the Service Act in the Slovenian Army. Thank you for your attention and a nice evening.

At the same time, the marriage proposal regulates compulsory supplementary pension insurance and payment of contributions also in the absence of work due to injury at work or occupational illness. Where soldiers are on duty on international military missions, a new system of daily and weekly rest is introduced. The change of the existing system is necessary as the regime currently in force does not allow soldiers to spend their days off. For the Chief of General Staff of the Slovenian Armed Forces, among other things, provision is made for early retirement without a reduction in pension. The proposal for this law is understood as a deliberate start to efforts to strengthen the attractiveness of the military profession. Twice the time of service of the military as a grant may be too long for many. We must not forget that in most cases it is the young students who opt for a scholarship. In doing so, he carried out his basic job of saving lives, so in the Desus Group we support the proposal that the soldier is also protected and protected during the journey. In the Desus Group we will support the proposed law.
